🔊 VSA readings · US close Mon 28 Sep 2026research, not advice
Volume Spread Analysis (Tom Williams, Gavin Holmes) reads each bar by its spread (range vs the last 20 bars), close (high / mid / low of the bar) and volume (vs the last 20 bars), against the background. Every sign is weighted by its context (trend, background, location): e.g. no demand inside a strong uptrend is usually just a pause, in a downtrend it is significant. Run over 628 desk names (plus a compact read of all 2749 RS-universe names for the charts): every setup in the scanners (VCP, SEPA, power play, HTF, flat base, Wyckoff springs when present) plus the tracked names, each over its own base / range window. Context only: VSA never creates, removes or re-scores a setup or a spring. Tap a ticker for its report card; tap "story" for the narrative.

Weight (0.3 = minor … 3.0 = major; colour = polarity, green strength / red weakness). Hover a cell for the reason and page reference. n/a = the sign is only defined in the other contexts (a test in a rising market needs an uptrend; a buying climax needs a prior rise).

📖 Signs & method

SCSelling climax (strength)
SVStopping volume (strength)
BHBag holding (strength)
SOShakeout (strength)
TTest (strength)
TTest in a rising market (strength)
NSNo supply (strength)
EREffort to rise (strength)
ABAbsorption volume (strength)
BCBuying climax (weakness)
UTUpthrust (weakness)
PUPseudo-upthrust (weakness)
NDNo demand (weakness)
EMEnd of a rising market (weakness)
EFEffort to fall (weakness)
FTPossible failed test (supply present) (weakness)
SQSquat bar (high volume, narrow spread) (context)

Bar measures: spread = high−low ÷ average of the prior 20 bars (wide ≥ 1.25×, narrow ≤ 0.75×); close position 0 (low) – 1 (high); volume ÷ prior 20-bar average (ultra high ≥ 2×, high ≥ 1.3×, low ≤ 0.8×, ultra low ≤ 0.5×); up/down bar = close vs prior close. Key rules: selling climax = wide down bar to a new 20-bar low on ≥ 2× volume after a decline, closing off the lows (or next bar up); stopping volume = heavy down bar in a reaction closing in its upper half, any spread (meaning set by trend: bullish in an uptrend, only a pause in a downtrend); no supply / no demand = narrow down / up bar on volume below the prior two bars; test = dips under the prior low, closes in the top 40% on low volume; test in a rising market (Holmes) = narrow down bar closing on the low on very low volume inside the prior bar, in an uptrend; upthrust = new short-term high, wide spread, closes in the lower quarter; buying climax = wide up bar into new highs on ≥ 2× volume closing mid/low after a run; end of a rising market = narrow up bar on ≥ 2× volume at new highs. Context brain: every sign is read against the trend at the bar before it (50-day slope, price vs 50-day, 20- vs 50-day, swing highs/lows; a ±15% 20-bar move overrides), the background (a climactic strength / weakness sign in the prior 30 bars) and the location (near range support / resistance, new 20-bar highs / lows). The context matrix above gives each sign × context a weight, polarity and reason (Williams / Holmes page refs); significance = bar match × weight × location. One context modifier: no demand in a downtrend / weak background followed within 5 bars by an upthrust is raised ×1.6 (Holmes p.81). Multi-bar sequences are Phase 2 and switched off. Balance 0–100 = recency-weighted sum of context-weighted signs plus the up-vs-down-bar volume ratio (≥ 60 strength, ≤ 40 weakness). Thresholds are desk calibration (Williams/Holmes publish none); pseudo-upthrust and squat are marked unverified in the method file. Confirmation uses the next bar, so older signs carry one bar of hindsight; today's bar is "awaiting next bar".

Sources: Tom Williams, Master the Markets (TradeGuider) and That Was The Week; Gavin Holmes, Trading in the Shadow of the Smart Money (page refs in the matrix; rules paraphrased). Price data to 2026-09-28; setup windows from the scan of 2026-09-28.
